/// Structural admission of `on_delete` (the compiler states each refusal first).
pub fn validate(schema: &Schema) -> Vec<SchemaIssue> {
    let mut issues = Vec::new();
    for (name, table) in &schema.tables {
        for (column, action) in &table.on_delete {
            let path = format!("tables.{name}.onDelete.{column}");
            let mut fail = |message: String| issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.clone(), message });
            let Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: parent }) = table.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required) else {
                fail(format!("on delete names {column}, which is not a reference column")); continue;
            };
            if table.written_by.contains_key(column) {
                fail(format!("{name}.{column} is field-owned; a private value carries no delete action"));
                continue;
            }
            if !schema.actionable(name) || !schema.actionable(parent) {
                fail(format!("{name}.{column} -> {parent} cannot carry a delete action: ephemeral, engine-owned and receipt or timeline bookkeeping tables keep their own lifetimes"));
                continue;
            }
            let through = table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| &t.reference == column);
            if action.acts() && schema.reference_index(name, column).is_none() {
                fail(format!("{name}.{column} needs its reference index (leading with {column})"));
            }
            if action.writes() {
                if delete_reachable(schema, parent) && schema.reference_cap(name, column).is_none() {
                    fail(format!("E_CASCADE_BOUND: {} on {name}.{column} needs `cap N by {column}`", action.words()));
                }
                if let Some(reason) = write_target_refusal(schema, name) {
                    fail(format!("{} on {name}.{column}: {reason}", action.words()));
                }
            }
            match action {
                OnDelete::Keep if through => fail(format!("on delete keep on read-through {name}.{column}: a read-through child lives within its parent")),
                OnDelete::SetNull => if let Some(reason) = set_null_refusal(schema, name, table, column) { fail(format!("on delete set null on {name}.{column}: {reason}")); },
                OnDelete::Cascade if through && table.is_expiring() => fail(format!("on delete cascade on read-through {name}.{column} needs a child that never expires")),
                _ => {}
            }
        }
    }
    if let Some(table) = schema.deletion_cycle() {
        issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("tables.{table}"), message: format!("{table} reaches itself through on delete cascade and read through: no order can delete those rows") });
    }
    // A missing reservation can mean an uncapped dormant descendant. Only
    // the structural graph walk above proves a cycle.
    issues
}

/// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#10 — only a live delete source requires action bounds.
/// Authored/system program deletes are checked separately by IR admission.
pub fn delete_source(schema: &Schema, name: &str) -> bool {
    schema.table(name).is_some_and(|table| table.is_expiring() || table.rules.delete != crate::schema::RuleExpr::Deny)
}

pub fn delete_reachable(schema: &Schema, name: &str) -> bool {
    let mut reached = schema.tables.keys().filter(|name| delete_source(schema, name)).cloned().collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    let mut pending = reached.iter().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>();
    while let Some(parent) = pending.pop() {
        for reference in schema.referencing(&parent) {
            if reference.action == OnDelete::Cascade && reached.insert(reference.child.to_owned()) {
                pending.push(reference.child.to_owned());
            }
        }
    }
    reached.contains(name)
}

# Security invariants

State privacy intent separately from enforcement. Check reports proved, refuted
with a replayed counterexample, or unproved with a reason; passing finite tests
are not proofs.

```quarry
table privateNotes:
  patient: principal
  body: text 1..2000
  sealed: bool default false
  read <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  insert <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  online only
-- Spec: a patient may see only their own note; sealing may narrow availability.
-- Removing an accidental broad branch from the rule must not change this intent.
invariant privateNotes.read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.body read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.insert only .patient = viewer
query privateNote(note: privateNotes):
  return privateNotes[note]
mutation writePrivateNote(body: text 1..2000):
  return insert privateNotes { patient: viewer, body }
```

Run `snapback4 check` → `snapback4 test` → `snapback4 audit`. Check reports
invariant counts and five actionable notes; `--verbose` retains every reason.
A mirror invariant cannot catch a wrong rule: the advisory asks
for the spec's audience, resource relationship and lifecycle condition instead.
A legitimate simple owner policy can be equivalent; the advisory never refuses it.
Review `snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft`: it offers unconfirmed
per-role/per-column alternatives and questions, never auto-accepted policy.

Test synthesizes two users per discovered role/seat and related ownership rows
without fixture files, including password-only apps. These private in-memory
fixtures never open dev doors. Every failure labels its initial fixture origin:
`generated-fixture-trace` means an authored observation from a validated generated
initial state, not proof that app operations created that state. Author-supplied
fixtures retain `authored-fixture-trace`. Both fail acceptance on actual observed
violations; seed-only/synthetic table probes remain non-failing supplemental evidence.
Fixture-free tests report that no authored business cases ran. Finite passing
campaigns never prove security. Check bounds concrete exploration to 512 call/world
reservations and 1500 ms; exhaustion stays unproved and points to the full test.
`audit --json` retains all column audiences, grant writers, exclusions and uncovered
role/row/effect exposures. Declaring one effect does not cover the others.

## Reference

`invariant table.column read only P` protects a column value, derived values and value-dependent counts/existence/refusals. An authored projection excluding the column does not by itself disclose that value; filtering, ordering or copying it can. Audit JSON includes `column_audiences` (effective row rule plus private-owner gate). Unknown derived and opaque paths remain unproved.

`invariant table.read only P` protects read intent; insert, update and delete
name authority. `invariant table.column write only P` covers writes to a
column (both insert and update). `invariant table.column update only P` checks
only edits, retaining old-and-next-image semantics; use it when creation is permitted
to a different role (for example counter staff creating a ticket price). `invariant table always P` states row integrity.
An update policy over `.field` constrains **both old and next images**.
A policy saying status must not be override therefore also restricts editing
a row that was already override, even when changing it away from that status.
For the role authority of one named mutation, use operation-specific intent
(`invariant table.operation only P`) instead. General next-image-only and
cross-operation transition assertions still need a separate reduction.
Predicates use the existing rule vocabulary: `.field`, `viewer`, `exists`,
comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`. For a role write use the complete roster probe,
for example `invariant members.role write only exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']`
in a schema declaring those tables. A declaration never grants authority. Whole-row read intent also protects logical
existence: when occupancy totals are intentionally public but roster identities
are private, declare column read intent for those identities instead. Include
a service seat explicitly when the spec permits it; seats are ordinary checked
viewers, never implicitly exempt.

The implemented proof fragment exhaustively checks independent three-valued
rule predicates. A failed implication is unproved until an engine replay
establishes a counterexample. A schema-valid seeded snapshot is not a claim
that an application operation sequence reaches that state. Required-column
non-null assertions have a separate validation theorem. Read/count/sum/existence obligations are reported separately. A maintained aggregate with bare privacy keys can prove its contributor audience; a broader aggregate can yield a paired present/absent counterexample. Exact server `not before`/`expire at` bounds can prove a temporal read component independently of physical sweeps. General transitions and unsupported expressions remain unproved.
Read-through captures/live parents, complex derived aggregates, raw rule probes,
owned-field update bypasses, cascade/set-null and opaque jobs/workers need
separate reductions. An unresolved path blocks the whole declaration's proof.

Server delivery authorizes against the current server snapshot. A disconnected
device can retain an earlier snapshot until it receives a revocation; even
`until-revoked` cannot know a later server role. Synced current-role intent is
therefore unproved for device access. Retained history follows its leave policy.
These reports exclude physical timing and resource/meter side channels and
explicitly exclude the trusted system-principal boundary. Service seats remain
inside the ordinary-viewer check; there is no implicit seat exemption.

## Current roles and derived grants

A membership or assignment created while a user is an inspector can survive
their demotion. Conjoin the **current reader's role** with the assignment
branch, or revoke every derived grant transactionally whenever the role changes.
A grant writer's role does not establish the recipient's current authority.
With independent current-role intent, grant-lifetime advice points at this pattern; the argument-grant advice identifies
arguments written into reader-grant columns: validate the chosen principal's
role and relationship, even when the caller is privileged.
Global admin authority must come from a trusted operator or existing admin;
a group-creation `created` probe must remain scoped to that group everywhere.
`N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION` identifies role assignments trusted by later rules without independent delegation. Open enrollment may be intentional: compare each note with the specification. See `guide staging` for operator first-admin setup and `guide sharing` for parent authority.

## Required evidence and deadlines

If the spec requires a photo, use a required `image <=5000000` column or
`[image <=5000000] 1..4`; `?` permits omission and `[image] <=4` permits zero.
Uploads are verified media and asset reads inherit row and private-field rules.
Optional-evidence advice asks the builder to check intentional optionality.
Text `min..max` counts non-whitespace Unicode characters toward the minimum;
the maximum counts all characters. Stored text is not trimmed or rewritten.
A sweep changes state eventually: compare the deadline with server `now` in
the mutation too if writes must stop exactly at that instant.

## Existence observations

A lookup that says NOT_FOUND on absence followed by a denial saying FORBIDDEN
can reveal existence to callers admitted by a broader read rule. Use the same
code **and message** for hidden/absent/denied cases when existence is private,
or tighten the table read rule. Program `exists` is caller-filtered; probes
inside table rules inspect raw rows and can disclose predicates indirectly.
Inspect both paths in `audit`; a payload-only read check is insufficient.

## When an observer remains unproved

The report names the edge that lacks a reduction. Preserve the specification
while changing its enforcement; weakening an invariant to match a leaking
rule does not repair privacy.

- Maintained counts/sums: retain privacy fields as ordinary bare group keys
  and protect the aggregate with the same audience predicate. Recorded
  reference paths, calendar keys, derived values, private columns and temporal
  contributors need their own reduction; their names are not source fields.
- Owner fields: declare column-write intent too. `written by` authorizes
  patches even when the ordinary update rule denies. To prove a particular
  mutation's role authority, use a dominating current-role requirement and
  operation-specific intent over a role table the mutation cannot change.
  This does not prove every owner-field or old/next-image path.
- Raw rule probes: inspect the observing table and its writers. A protected
  target's read rule does not protect predicates probed from another rule.
  Apply the intended audience at that observer, or remove the raw dependency;
  the report retains the unsupported edge until it can establish implication.
- Cascade/set-null: automatic child changes bypass child write rules. An
  explicit authorized child mutation with `restrict` removes that automatic
  writer, but parent refusal/existence and transitive references remain
  separate observations. Read-through/live-parent authority requires a separate reduction.
- Jobs/native workers/actions: the audit names the trusted or opaque writer.
  An ordinary mutation with stable role requirements can prove that mutation's
  authority; arbitrary native/worker output cannot acquire a proof from a
  caller's role or a passing finite test.
- Schedules: use a stored `not before`/`expire at` lifetime for exact read
  boundaries, or compare server `now` in the write itself. A scheduled status
  update leaves the old status visible between deadline and sweep.

Default `test` challenges pure-schema applications through a private production
`serve`: unauthenticated calls, forged persona headers and development doors.
The host uses no pre-existing developer credentials or development mode. Applications needing
native/worker artifacts are explicitly not covered by that schema-only host.
Focused fixtures also enable short ordered mutation histories followed by
ordinary reads; traces record actual calls and current policy evaluation.
Named personas/service seats and enum argument combinations are enumerated
within reported limits. Asset challenges use real foreign-upload and verified
non-image ledgers; video arguments and unvisited histories remain named. Complete fixture stores preserve image/file ledgers, captures, read-through deadlines, maintained views and expiry. Histories include fixture principals and reference-row candidates, under a reported work cap. Neither
these finite campaigns nor an offline device's retained snapshot establishes
current-server revocation at a disconnected access.

### E_SECURITY_TARGET

E_SECURITY_TARGET means the invariant target does not name a declared table, column or effect. Correct the target name; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SECURITY_INTENT

N_SECURITY_INTENT means access rules lack independent declared intent. Declare policy from the spec; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_BROAD_READ

N_BROAD_READ identifies universal or roster-wide readership; its message states the exact condition. Confirm the intended audience; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE means a stored grant may survive a later role change. Recheck the current reader role; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_ARGUMENT_GRANT

N_ARGUMENT_GRANT means an argument becomes a reader-grant column. Validate the recipient role and relationship; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE

N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE means optional media permits omitted evidence. Declare required media when the spec requires it; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE

N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE means sweeps leave time between a deadline and cleanup. Enforce the deadline in the mutation; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE

N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE means absence and later denial can reveal different outcomes. Unify the code and message when existence is private; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

The isolated production HTTP campaign also signs in, refreshes the session,
logs out all sessions and retries both retired credentials against query,
mutation, schema, changes, sync, probe and refresh doors. It reports missing coverage
when ordinary identity or complete deployed worker artifacts are unavailable.
This checks server admission; a disconnected device can still retain old bytes.

### N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies self-signup roles or role-grant operations that can confer authority trusted by other rules. Restrict assignment to authorized delegation; provision the first administrator with `guide staging`.

Identity rate refusals return HTTP `Retry-After` (seconds) and
`denied.retryAfterMs` (milliseconds), conservatively covering the 60-second
window. Wait that interval before retrying identity requests.

REL5 owns release transitions/old-client compatibility; MIG owns backfills and
staging clock control for day-two qualification.

### N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE

N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE means intent and the effective rule admit the same cases
in the bounded predicate kernel. Check the original requirement before rewriting; an equivalent policy
may be correct, but cannot detect a mistake copied into both declarations.

### N_ROLE_WIDE_READ

N_ROLE_WIDE_READ means a role-wide branch gated by a row value exposes every column
on matching rows.
Use a board/projection table for a public rating; declare intent for the private
notes, principals, images and files separately.

        // Upsert mints its insert id; if-absent may instead supply one.

        if if_absent && let Some(index) = proof.or(self.device.then_some("byId")) {
            self.prove_upsert_insert(table_name, index, &row)?;
        }
        // An upsert has already resolved this exact address (or minted the id)
        // and reserved both target proofs. Retiring an expired predecessor
        // uses that same allowance, not a third read-rule reservation.

        let has_through_children = self.schema.children(table_name).next().is_some();
        let has_children = has_through_children || self.schema.is_referenced(table_name);
        let predecessor = self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, collision_probes)], |runner, allowance| {
            let existing = runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, &id)?;
            if let Some(row) = &existing {
                if !runner.expired(table, row) { runner.visible_collision(table_name, row, Effect::Insert, "unique id")?; }
                // An expired predecessor's read rule cannot veto actionable
                // reference retirement. A declared read-through child is
                // different: a non-reader cannot probe whether hidden
                // physical children retain the predecessor.
                else if has_through_children && !runner.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, row)? {
                    return Err(runner.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "unique id"));
                }
            }
            Ok(existing)
        })?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.4 — a logical device miss cannot prove there is
        // no hidden, expired physical predecessor whose retirement would run
        // reference actions. Controller-minted identities are the existing
        // fresh-address proof; every other referenced same-id insert queues.
        if self.device && predecessor.is_none() && has_children && table.is_expiring() && !minted {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!(
                "same-id insert into referenced {table_name} needs the server's physical predecessor check; queued without predicted rows"
            )));
        }

            let mark = self.meter.mark();
            if self.physical_children(table_name, &Value::Id(id.clone()))? {
                // The bounded probes have completed. Their occupancy is not
                // disclosed by the collision's logical meter or heap peak.
                self.meter.restore(mark);
                return Err(if predecessor.is_some() {
                    RuntimeError::Constraint(format!("{table_name} already has a row with id {id}"))
                } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "parent identity has stored children") });
            }
        }
        if let Some(existing) = &predecessor {

                return Err(RuntimeError::Constraint(format!("{table_name} already has a row with id {id}")));
            }
            // The expired predecessor is internal authorization work, not a
            // disclosed examination (LLP 3000.015 §§1–2).
            self.retire_expired_row(table_name, existing)?;
        }
        // Retiring an expired predecessor can cascade a source row whose
        // contribution determines this image's maintained columns. Initialize
        // them from the post-retirement private totals. Maintained columns are
        // forbidden from constraints and authority, so the earlier collision
        // and authorization preflights need only the authored image.

        if let Some(index) = proof.filter(|_| {

            // Otherwise the proof's own unknown fact is not the prediction's.

                let beyond = self.beyond_horizon;
                let proved = self.device_work(|runner| runner.prove_upsert_insert(table_name, index, &row));
                (self.beyond_horizon, self.missing_fact) = (beyond, None);
                match proved { Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(_)) => self.unproved_write = true, other => other? }
            }
        }
        // Retirement released every logical key and cap slot. Treat the new
        // image as a fresh insert: an unchanged secondary key can now belong
        // to another live row and must be probed again.
        self.check_constraints(table_name, None, &row, Effect::Insert)?;
        self.commit_sequences(table_name, &row)?;
        self.commit_receipt_counter(table_name, &row)?;
        let row = self.prepare_event_slots(table_name, None, row, image_bytes)?;
        validate_row(self.schema, table_name, &row)?;
        let entries = self.maintain_scratch(self.contribution_heap(table), |runner| {
            runner.tx.put(table_name, table, &row, None)
        })?;
        self.charge_source_indexes(table, None, Some(&row), entries)?;
        self.row_changed(table_name, None, Some(&row), Origin::Source)?;
        Ok(true)
    }

//! Parent authority and lifetime checks shared by the storage adapters.
// @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — reserve before arm selection; resolve live, stamp, then authorize.
use super::{rules, runner::Runner};
use crate::{
    error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError},
    rows::{Bounds, Dir},
    schema::{Effect, Table, through::*},
    value::{Supplied, Value},
};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

/// Minted only here: the stamped image re-enters the supplied-input channel
/// under reserve_capture's declared allowance.
pub(crate) struct CaptureInput(());

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) enum ParentVerdict {
    Readable,
    Unreadable,
    Absent,
    Unknown,
}

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn reserve_capture(&mut self, table: &Table) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let Some(through) = &table.through else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        if self.capture_prepaid {
            return Ok(());
        }
        let parent = &self.schema.tables[&through.parent];
        self.charge_price(crate::schema::reservations::capture_price(self.schema, table))?;
        self.capture_prepaid = true;
        self.read_tables.insert(through.parent.clone());
        self.rule_dependencies(&parent.rules.read);
        self.rule_dependencies(&table.rules.insert);
        Ok(())
    }

    pub(super) fn capture_insert(&mut self, name: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<Supplied, RuntimeError> {
        let result = self.capture_insert_inner(name, row);
        self.trace_authorization(&result, name, Effect::Insert, "read through capture");
        result
    }

    fn capture_insert_inner(
        &mut self,
        name: &str,
        row: Supplied,
    ) -> Result<Supplied, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        let Some(through) = &table.through else {
            return Ok(row);
        };
        let mut row = row.into_value();
        let id = row
            .field(&through.reference)
            .ok()
            .and_then(|v| v.as_str().ok())
            .ok_or_else(|| self.rule_refusal(name, Effect::Insert, "read through parent reference"))?;
        let parent = &self.schema.tables[&through.parent];
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — the parent lookup is authorization work under the
        // request's physical allowance; reserve_capture prepaid its logical charges.
        let found = self.authorized_scan(&[], |runner, scope| runner.tx.get(scope, &through.parent, id))?.filter(|r| !self.expired(parent, r));
        if self.device {
            let covered = if let Some(found) = &found {
                self.is_provisional(&through.parent, found)? || self.device_work(|runner| {
                    let viewer = runner.context.viewer.clone();
                    let now = runner.context.now.unwrap_or(0);
                    runner.authorized_scan(&[], |runner, scope| runner.tx.recording_covered(&through.parent, found, &viewer, now, scope, &mut runner.meter))
                })?
            } else { false };
            if !covered { return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("read through .{} needs a live, covered {} parent; queued without a row", through.reference, through.parent))); }
        }
        let found = found.ok_or_else(|| self.rule_refusal(name, Effect::Insert, "read through parent missing or expired"))?;
        stamp(parent, through, &found, &mut row)?;
        table.recompute_deadline(&mut row)?;
        if !self.context.is_system() {
            let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
            let readable = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read), &[], |runner, allowance| {
                rules::evaluate(&table.rules.read, &viewer, None, &row, false, &mut super::read::PrefixProbe { runner, allowance })
            })?;
            if !readable {
                // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#5 — only the developer trace names the unread parent.
                self.refusing_phase(&through.parent, &format!("insert into {name} needs unread parent {} through .{}", through.parent, through.reference), "read rule");
                return Err(RuntimeError::Rule { table: name.into(), effect: Effect::Insert });
            }
        }
        Ok(row.capture_input(&CaptureInput(())))
    }

    pub(super) fn physical_children(
        &mut self,
        parent: &str,
        id: &Value,
    ) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.physical_children_outside(parent, id, &BTreeSet::new())
    }

    /// Whether a read-through child remains outside a delete's cascade
    /// closure. Children in `deleting` are removed first by deletion_order and
    /// therefore do not physically retain their parent.
    pub(super) fn physical_children_outside(
        &mut self,
        parent: &str,
        id: &Value,
        deleting: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>,
    ) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let children: Vec<_> = self
            .schema
            .children(parent)
            .map(|(n, _, _)| {
                // Only identities from this child table can appear in its
                // index scan. Identities from other closure tables cannot
                // consume a row in this table's prefix.
                let inside = deleting.iter().filter(|(table, _)| table == n).count();
                (n.clone(), inside.saturating_add(1))
            })
            .collect();
        // Reserve the complete schema-fixed probe set before its first seek.
        // Never short-circuit on an occupied table, including at low ceilings.
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — the seeks draw from the request's physical allowance; occupancy never selects the logical charge.
        let heap = children.iter().map(|(name, limit)| super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(&self.schema.tables[name]).saturating_mul(*limit as u64)).fold(0_u64, u64::saturating_add);
        let probes = children.iter().map(|(_, limit)| *limit as u64).fold(0_u64, u64::saturating_add);
        self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::Examined, probes), (MeterKind::Steps, probes), (MeterKind::HeapBytes, heap)], |runner, scope| {
            let mut occupied = false;
            for (name, limit) in &children {
                let rows = runner.tx.scan(scope, name, CHILDREN, &[id.clone()], &Bounds::default(), Dir::Asc, *limit)?;
                occupied |= rows.into_iter().any(|(row, _)| row.field("id").ok().and_then(|id| id.as_str().ok())
                    .is_none_or(|child| !deleting.contains(&(name.clone(), child.to_owned()))));
            }
            Ok(occupied)
        })
    }

    // A deadline suffix skips an arbitrary expired backlog in one seek.
    pub(super) fn refuse_live_children(
        &mut self,
        parent: &str,
        id: &Value,
    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let children: Vec<_> = self
            .schema
            .children(parent)
            .map(|(n, _, _)| n.clone())
            .collect();
        if children.is_empty() { return Ok(()); }
        let now = self
            .context
            .now
            .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("delete clock is not pinned".into()))?;
        let heap = children
            .iter()
            .map(|n| super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(&self.schema.tables[n]).saturating_mul(3))
            .fold(0_u64, u64::saturating_add);
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — three schema-fixed seeks per child table, each drawn from the request's physical allowance.
        self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::Examined, children.len() as u64 * 3), (MeterKind::Steps, children.len() as u64 * 3), (MeterKind::HeapBytes, heap)], |runner, scope| {
            if runner.device {
                let occupied = runner.device_work(|runner| runner.current_children(parent, id, &children))?;
                return if occupied { Err(runner.parent_has_children(parent)) } else { Ok(()) };
            }
            for name in &children {
                let never = runner.tx.scan(scope, name, CHILDREN, &[id.clone(), Value::Null], &Bounds::default(), Dir::Asc, 1)?;
                let finite = runner.tx.scan(scope, name, CHILDREN, &[id.clone()], &Bounds { gt: Some(Value::Int(now)), ..Default::default() }, Dir::Asc, 1)?;
                if !never.is_empty() || !finite.is_empty() {
                    return Err(runner.parent_has_children(parent));
                }
                // §3.3 forbids physical removal even when §6's live probe is empty.
                // The author's implementation ruling keeps this case fail-closed.
                if !runner.tx.scan(scope, name, CHILDREN, &[id.clone()], &Bounds::default(), Dir::Asc, 1)?.is_empty() {
                    return Err(runner.parent_has_children(parent));
                }
            }
            Ok(())
        })
    }

        RuntimeError::Structural {

//! Real operation histories from a complete trusted fixture, including assets.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#122-reproducible-campaigns-and-reachability — only actual fixture traces establish application reachability.
use super::*;
const LIMIT:usize=512;
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize)]
pub struct Step {pub operation:String,pub viewer:String,pub args:BTreeMap<String,Value>,pub result:serde_json::Value}

pub(super) fn viewers(bound:&Bound)->Vec<String>{
    let mut out=BTreeSet::from([VIEWER.into(),OTHER.into(),"account:security-no-role".into(),"service:security".into()]);
    out.extend(bound.personas.iter().map(|p|if p.contains(':'){p.clone()}else{format!("dev:{p}")}));
    fn seats(value:&serde_json::Value,out:&mut BTreeSet<String>){match value{
        serde_json::Value::String(s) if s.starts_with("service:")&&s.len()>8=>{out.insert(s.clone());},
        serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>for x in xs{seats(x,out)},
        serde_json::Value::Object(xs)=>for x in xs.values(){seats(x,out)},_=>{}
    }}
    seats(&serde_json::to_value(&bound.schema).unwrap(),&mut out);
    for p in &bound.programs{seats(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),&mut out);}
    seats(&serde_json::to_value(&bound.invariants).unwrap(),&mut out);
    out.into_iter().collect()
}
fn actors(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>)->Vec<String>{
    let mut out=viewers(bound);
    for (table,rs) in rows{for row in rs{for (c,k) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns{
        if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)&&let Ok(Value::Id(p))=row.field(c)&&!out.contains(p){out.push(p.clone());}
    }}}
    out
}
pub(super) fn fixture_cases(p:&Program,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String],out:&mut Campaign)->Vec<BTreeMap<String,Value>>{
    let args=&p.args;
    let mut literals=BTreeMap::<String,Vec<Value>>::new();
    fn collect(value:&serde_json::Value,out:&mut BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>){
        if let Some(binary)=value.get("Binary") {for (a,b) in [("left","right"),("right","left")] {
            if let (Some(name),Some(literal))=(binary.get(a).and_then(|v|v.get("Variable")).and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str),binary.get(b).and_then(|v|v.get("Literal"))) {
                if let Ok(value)=serde_json::from_value::<Value>(literal.clone()){out.entry(name.into()).or_default().push(value);}
            }
        }}
        match value{serde_json::Value::Object(xs)=>for v in xs.values(){collect(v,out)},serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>for v in xs{collect(v,out)},_=>{}}
    }
    collect(&serde_json::to_value(p).unwrap(),&mut literals);
    fn values(name:&str,k:&ir::ArgType,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String])->Option<Vec<Value>>{
        Some(match k{
            ir::ArgType::Enum{variants}=>variants.iter().map(|s|Value::String(s.clone())).collect(),
            ir::ArgType::Int=>vec![Value::Int(0),Value::Int(1),Value::Int(2)],
            ir::ArgType::IntRange{min,max}=>{let mut xs=vec![*min,1i64.clamp(*min,*max),*max];xs.sort();xs.dedup();xs.into_iter().map(Value::Int).collect()},
            ir::ArgType::Bool=>vec![Value::Bool(false),Value::Bool(true)],
            ir::ArgType::Time=>vec![Value::Int(NOW-86_400_000),Value::Int(NOW),Value::Int(NOW+86_400_000)],
            ir::ArgType::Principal=>actors.iter().map(|s|Value::Id(s.clone())).collect(),
            ir::ArgType::Ref{table} if rows.get(table).is_some_and(|rs|!rs.is_empty())=>rows[table].iter().filter_map(|r|r.field("id").ok().cloned()).collect(),
            ir::ArgType::Optional(inner)=>{let mut v=vec![Value::Null];v.extend(values(name,inner,rows,actors)?);v},
            _=>vec![arguments(&BTreeMap::from([(name.into(),k.clone())]),0)?.remove(name)?],
        })
    }
    let mut cases=vec![BTreeMap::new()];
    for (name,k) in args{
        let Some(mut vals)=values(name,k,rows,actors)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{name}: history needs a typed asset argument"));return Vec::new();};
        for literal in literals.get(name).into_iter().flatten() {
            let valid=match (k,literal){
                (ir::ArgType::Text{min,max,..},Value::String(s))=>s.chars().filter(|c|!c.is_whitespace()).count()>=*min as usize&&s.chars().count()<=*max as usize,
                (ir::ArgType::Int,Value::Int(_))=>true,
                (ir::ArgType::IntRange{min,max},Value::Int(n))=>(*min..=*max).contains(n),_=>false,
            };
            if valid&&!vals.contains(literal){vals.insert(0,literal.clone());}
        }
        let mut next=Vec::new();
        'product:for offset in 0..vals.len(){for n in 0..cases.len().max(vals.len()){
            if out.exhausted(){return next;}
            let mut case=cases[n%cases.len()].clone();case.insert(name.clone(),vals[(n+offset)%vals.len()].clone());
            if !next.contains(&case){next.push(case);}if next.len()==LIMIT{out.unsupported.insert("history arguments capped at 512 combinations".into());break 'product;}
        }}
        cases=next;
    }cases
}
fn violation(out:&mut Campaign,inv:&Invariant,steps:&[Step],viewer:&str,row:&Value,old:Option<&Value>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,effect:&str){
    let committed=|steps:&[Step]|steps.iter().filter(|s|s.result.get("state").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)==Some("sent")).count();
    if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()&&v.viewer==viewer&&v.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace&&committed(&v.history)==committed(steps)){return;}
    out.violations.push(Counterexample{reachability:Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace,observations:None,history:steps.to_vec(),invariant_site:inv.site.to_string(),args:BTreeMap::new(),viewer:viewer.into(),operation:steps.last().map(|s|s.operation.clone()).unwrap_or("$fixture.read".into()),effect:effect.into(),row:row.clone(),old:old.cloned(),supporting_rows:initial.clone(),rule_path:"actual authored operation/read from the trusted fixture".into(),scope:"reachable from recorded trusted fixture; finite trace, not an all-history proof".into()});
}
fn typed_disclosure(ty:&crate::types::Ty,data:&Value,table:&str,key:&Value,column:Option<&str>,physical:&Value)->bool{
    use crate::types::Ty;
    let payload=|data:&Value|matches!(data,Value::Row(_))&&data.field("id").ok()==Some(key)&&column.is_none_or(|c|data.field(c).ok().is_some_and(|v|physical.field(c).ok()==Some(v)));
    match ty{
        Ty::Row(t)|Ty::WriteRow(t) if t==table=>payload(data),
        Ty::Optional(inner)=>typed_disclosure(inner,data,table,key,column,physical),
        Ty::Page(t,_) if t==table=>matches!(data,Value::Array(xs) if xs.iter().any(payload)),
        Ty::List(inner,_)|Ty::Collection(inner,_)=>matches!(data,Value::Array(xs) if xs.iter().any(|r|typed_disclosure(inner,r,table,key,column,physical))),
        Ty::Object(fields)|Ty::RowShape(fields)|Ty::RowObject(fields)=>fields.iter().any(|(name,ty)|data.field(name).ok().is_some_and(|v|typed_disclosure(ty,v,table,key,column,physical))),
        _=>false,
    }
}
fn typed_source(ty:&crate::types::Ty,table:&str)->bool{
    use crate::types::Ty;
    match ty{Ty::Row(t)|Ty::WriteRow(t)|Ty::Page(t,_)=>t==table,Ty::Optional(inner)|Ty::List(inner,_)|Ty::Collection(inner,_)=>typed_source(inner,table),Ty::Object(fields)|Ty::RowShape(fields)|Ty::RowObject(fields)=>fields.values().any(|ty|typed_source(ty,table)),_=>false}
}
pub(super) fn observer_tables(bound:&Bound,table:&str)->BTreeSet<String>{
    let mut out=BTreeSet::from([table.into()]);
    loop{let before=out.len();
        for m in &bound.schema.maintains{if out.contains(&m.source){out.insert(m.target.clone());if let Some(owner)=&m.private{out.insert(owner.table.clone());}}}
        for (name,t) in &bound.schema.tables{if t.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|edge|out.contains(&edge.parent))||[&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|kernel::dependencies(r).iter().any(|t|out.contains(t))){out.insert(name.clone());}}
        if out.len()==before{return out;}
    }
}
fn reserve_observers(out:&mut Campaign,calls:usize)->bool{
    if !out.reserve(calls){return false;}
    if out.history_observer_calls+calls>32768{out.unsupported.insert("history read observers capped at 32768 calls; remaining disclosure combinations not covered".into());false}else{out.history_observer_calls+=calls;true}
}
fn observe(bound:&Bound,e:&mut Engine,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String],steps:&[Step],out:&mut Campaign){
    if !reserve_observers(out,0)||out.history_observer_calls>=32768{return;}
    let queries=bound.programs.iter().filter(|bp|bp.program.kind==ProgramKind::Query&&!bp.program.native).filter(|bp|{
        let json=serde_json::to_string(&bp.program).unwrap();
        if ["NewId","Emit","JobAs","JobQuery"].iter().any(|tag|json.contains(tag)){
            out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: generated-id/opaque history observer needs disclosure provenance",bp.program.name));false
        }else{true}
    }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"){
        let direct=inv.policy.as_ref().is_some_and(|p|kernel::implies(&super::super::columns::admission(&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table],inv.column.as_deref()),p)==Ok(true));
        let indirect=bound.schema.maintains.iter().any(|m|m.source==inv.table)||bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|[&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|kernel::dependencies(r).contains(&inv.table)));
        if direct&&!indirect{continue;}

        let observing=observer_tables(bound,&inv.table);
        for row in rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){'actors:for viewer in actors{
            if out.exhausted(){return;}
            let committed=steps.iter().filter(|s|s.result.get("state").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)==Some("sent")).count();
            if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()&&v.viewer==*viewer&&v.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace&&v.history.iter().filter(|s|s.result.get("state").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)==Some("sent")).count()==committed){continue;}
            if !reserve_observers(out,1){return;}
            match policy_in(bound,inv,rows,viewer,None,row,e){Ok(false)=>{},Ok(true)=>continue,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {why}",inv.target_label()));continue;}}
            let Ok(key)=row.field("id")else{continue;};
            if inv.column.as_ref().and_then(|c|bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].written_by.get(c)).is_some_and(|gate|!gate.audience&&row.field(&gate.owner).ok()!=Some(&Value::Id(viewer.clone()))){continue 'actors;}

            // A typed authored row return is already a payload witness. It does
            // not require an invalid absent world that deletes referenced data.
            for bp in queries.iter().copied().filter(|p|typed_source(&p.result,&inv.table)){
                let cases=fixture_cases(&bp.program,rows,actors,out);
                if cases.len()>32{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: typed read-observer arguments capped at 32",bp.program.name));}
                for args in cases.into_iter().take(32){
                    if !reserve_observers(out,2){return;}
                    let args=match materialize_arguments(e,&bp.program.args,&args,viewer){Ok(a)=>a,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: typed history observer asset/input preparation: {why}",bp.program.name));continue;}};
                    out.attempted+=1;
                    if let Ok(q)=e.query(&bp.program.name,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW))&&!q.unavailable&&typed_disclosure(&bp.result,&q.data,&inv.table,key,inv.column.as_deref(),row){
                        out.attempted+=1;
                        if !matches!(e.query(&bp.program.name,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)),Ok(again) if !again.unavailable&&again.data==q.data){
                            out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: typed read payload is not repeatable on the committed image",bp.program.name));continue;
                        }
                        violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,row,None,initial,if inv.column.is_some(){"column read observation"}else{"read"});
                        if let Some(v)=out.violations.last_mut(){v.operation=bp.program.name.clone();v.args=args;v.rule_path="authored query repeatably returned a compiler-typed row/column payload of the protected table while current policy is false".into();}
                        continue 'actors;
                    }
                }
            }
            if inv.column.as_deref().is_some_and(|c|c!="id"){continue 'actors;}
            if bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|t.columns.values().any(|k|matches!(k.required(),C::Ref{table} if table==&inv.table))){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: history paired absence needs a referentially valid closure",inv.table));continue;}
            let Ok(id)=key.as_str()else{continue;};
            if !reserve_observers(out,3){return;}
            let mut absent=match fork(bound,e,Vec::new()){Ok(e)=>e,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: absent world fork: {why}",inv.table));continue;}};
            if let Err(why)=absent.seed_delete_at(&inv.table,id,NOW){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: absent world refused: {why}",inv.table));continue;}
            if let Err(why)=validate_fixture(bound,&mut absent){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: absent world invalid: {why}",inv.table));continue;}
            let absent_rows=match readback(&absent){Ok(r)=>r,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: absent world readback: {why}",inv.table));continue;}};
            if policy_in(bound,inv,&absent_rows,viewer,None,row,&mut absent)!=Ok(false){continue;}
            for bp in queries.iter().copied().filter(|p|observing.iter().any(|t|super::super::audit::mentions(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),t))){
                let p=&bp.program;
                let cases=fixture_cases(p,rows,actors,out);
                if cases.len()>32{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: read-observer arguments capped at 32; remaining combinations not covered",p.name));}
                for args in cases.into_iter().take(32){
                    if !reserve_observers(out,4){return;}
                    let raw_args=args;
                    let args=match materialize_arguments(e,&p.args,&raw_args,viewer){Ok(a)=>a,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired history observer asset/input preparation: {why}",p.name));continue;}};
                    if args!=raw_args{
                        if !reserve_observers(out,2){return;}
                        absent=match fork(bound,e,Vec::new()){Ok(e)=>e,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired history argument ledger fork: {why}",p.name));continue;}};
                        if let Err(why)=absent.seed_delete_at(&inv.table,id,NOW){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired history argument ledger absence: {why}",p.name));continue;}
                        if let Err(why)=validate_fixture(bound,&mut absent){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: ledger absence world invalid: {why}",p.name));continue;}
                        if readback(&absent)!=Ok(absent_rows.clone()){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: paired argument ledger changes the absent base world",p.name));continue;}
                    }
                    out.attempted+=2;
                    let (Ok(a),Ok(b))=(paired::observe(e,p,viewer,args.clone()),paired::observe(&mut absent,p,viewer,args.clone()))else{continue;};
                    if a==b{continue;}

fn replay(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,word:&str,row:&Value)->Result<(bool,Value),String>{
    let name="$security.probe";let table=inv.table.clone();let key=id(&table);
    let read=Read{site:"security challenge".into(),table:table.clone(),index:"byId".into(),key:vec![Expr::Literal(key.clone())],provenance:KeyProvenance::Computed};
    let mut seeds=rows.clone();
    let probe=match word{
        "read"=>program(name,ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Get(read))]),
        "insert"=>{seeds.remove(&table);program(name,ProgramKind::Mutation,vec![Stmt::Insert{table,row:Expr::Literal(row.clone())},Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])},
        "delete"=>program(name,ProgramKind::Mutation,vec![Stmt::Delete{table,key:Expr::Literal(key)},Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))]),
        "update"=>{
            let definition=&bound.schema.tables[&table];
            let changed=definition.columns.iter().filter(|(n,_)|n.as_str()!="id"&&!definition.engine_field(n))
                .filter(|(n,_)|inv.column.as_ref().is_none_or(|c|c==*n))
                .filter(|(n,_)|!definition.constraints.iter().any(|c|matches!(c,Constraint::Immutable{fields} if fields.contains(n))))
                .find_map(|(n,k)| {let old=row.field(n).ok()?;let next=match (k.required(),old){
                    (C::Bool,Value::Bool(v))=>Value::Bool(!v),
                    (C::Enum{variants},_)=>Value::String(variants.iter().find(|v|Some(Value::String((*v).clone()))!=Some(old.clone()))?.clone()),
                    (C::Text{max},Value::String(v)) if v.chars().count()<*max as usize=>Value::String(format!("{v}x")),
                    (C::Int|C::Time,Value::Int(v))=>Value::Int(v+1),_=>return None};
                    Some((n.clone(),next))});
            let Some((column,value))=changed else{return Err("no valid changed mutable-column sample".into());};
            program(name,ProgramKind::Mutation,vec![Stmt::Update{table,key:Expr::Literal(key),patch:Expr::Literal(Value::Map([(column,value)].into_iter().collect()))},Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])
        },_=>return Err("row integrity/transition challenge is not an operation admission test".into()),
    };
    let mut engine=engine(bound,&seeds,probe)?;
    if word=="read"{return engine.query(name,Context::new(viewer,BTreeMap::new()).at(NOW)).map(|q|(q.data!=Value::Null,row.clone())).map_err(|e|e.to_string());}
    let outcome=engine.mutate(name,None,Context::new(viewer,BTreeMap::new()).at(NOW)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    if !matches!(outcome.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Sent{..}){return Ok((false,row.clone()));}
    let next=engine.store().rows(&inv.table).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.into_iter().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok());
    if word=="delete"{return if next.is_none(){Ok((true,row.clone()))}else{Err("delete returned sent without removing the target".into())};}
    let next=next.ok_or("sent write has no committed target image")?;
    if word=="update" {
        let table=&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table];
        let changed=table.columns.keys().filter(|c|!table.engine_field(c)&&!table.maintained.contains_key(*c))
            .any(|c|row.field(c).ok()!=next.field(c).ok()&&inv.column.as_ref().is_none_or(|column|column==c));
        if !changed{return Err("sent no-op/implicit-only update has no changed declared target column".into());}
    }
    Ok((true,next))
}
fn readback(engine:&Engine)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,String>{engine.schema().tables.keys().map(|t|engine.store().rows(t).map(|rs|(t.clone(),rs)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())).collect()}

fn operation_preflight(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant)->Result<(),String>{
    let Some(name)=&inv.operation else{return Ok(());};
    let p=&bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==name).ok_or("operation did not bind")?.program;
    if !super::census::body(&p.body){return Err(format!("{name}: opaque/loop/scan path requires write-boundary tracing"));}
    let rule=inv.policy.as_ref().ok_or("operation policy outside rule fragment")?;
    if rule.any_term(&|t|!matches!(t,RuleTerm::Viewer|RuleTerm::Literal(_))){return Err("operation policy requires write-boundary row-image tracing".into());}
    let writes=ir::write_tables(p);
    if writes.iter().filter(|(t,_)|t==&inv.table).count()>1{return Err(format!("{name}: multiple target writes need executed-effect tracing; net images do not identify delete/reinsert"));}
    let unstable=kernel::dependencies(rule).iter().any(|dep|{
        let hits=writes.iter().filter(|(t,_)|t==dep).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        !hits.is_empty()&&!(dep==&inv.table&&hits.len()==1&&hits[0].1=="insert")
    });
    if unstable||!bound.schema.maintains.is_empty()||bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|!t.sequences.is_empty()||!t.maintained.is_empty()||t.on_delete.values().any(|a|a.writes())){
        return Err("operation policy dependency may change before/after its write boundary; snapshot correspondence unproved".into());
    }Ok(())
}

/// Schema-only engine challenges. Successful synthetic operations refute the
/// declared table-authority contract, not application-operation reachability.
pub fn run(bound:&Bound)->Campaign{run_selected(bound,None)}
/// `check` searches unproved direct obligations; unsearched blockers remain
/// explicitly unproved. Default `test` passes None and runs the whole set.
pub fn run_selected(bound:&Bound,sites:Option<&BTreeSet<String>>)->Campaign{run_selected_after(bound,sites,Campaign::default())}
/// Continue after trusted observations, preserving one set of work budgets.
pub fn run_selected_after(bound:&Bound,sites:Option<&BTreeSet<String>>,mut out:Campaign)->Campaign{
    // A direct component theorem needs no repeated concrete search for that
    // same component. Indirect observers, temporal reads and actual authored
    // operations below still run; the whole declaration remains unproved.
    let discharged=super::analyze(bound).invariants.into_iter()
        .filter(|v|v.direct_rule==Status::Proved&&v.invariant.operation.is_none()
            && (matches!(v.invariant.target.as_str(),"read"|"always"|"insert"|"delete")
                || matches!(v.invariant.target.as_str(),"update"|"write")&&bound.schema.tables[&v.invariant.table].written_by.is_empty()))
        .map(|v|v.invariant.site.to_string()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    let mut assertions=bound.invariants.clone();
    for inv in &bound.invariants {
        if inv.target=="read"&&inv.column.as_deref().is_some_and(|c|c!="id"){continue;}
        if inv.operation.is_some()||matches!(inv.target.as_str(),"read"|"always"){continue;}
        for p in &bound.programs {if p.program.kind!=ProgramKind::Mutation{continue;}
            let relevant=ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(table,kind)|table==&inv.table&&(inv.target=="write"||inv.target==*kind||*kind=="upsert"));
            if relevant{let mut op=inv.clone();op.operation=Some(p.program.name.clone());assertions.push(op);}
        }
    }
    for inv in &assertions{
        if out.exhausted(){break;}
        if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()&&v.reachability.is_reachable()){continue;}
        if inv.target=="read"&&inv.column.as_deref().is_some_and(|c|c!="id"){continue;}
        if sites.is_some_and(|sites|!sites.contains(&inv.site.to_string())){continue;}
        if inv.operation.is_none()&&discharged.contains(&inv.site.to_string()){continue;}
        if let Err(why)=operation_preflight(bound,inv){out.unsupported.insert(why);continue;}
        let mut found=false;let mut seen=BTreeSet::new();
        let combinations=bound.schema.tables.values().flat_map(|t|t.columns.values()).filter_map(|k|if let C::Enum{variants}=k.required(){Some(variants.len())}else{None}).fold(1usize,usize::saturating_mul).max(24);
        if combinations>512{out.unsupported.insert("schema enum Cartesian matrix exceeds 512 samples; remaining tuples are not covered".into());}
        for sample in 0..=combinations.min(512) {
            if !out.reserve(1){break;}
            let variant=sample.saturating_sub(1);
            let mut rows=match snapshot(bound,inv,variant){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);break;}};
            // Try explicit policy field literals before the unchanged enum
            // matrix. A value such as status='cancelled' need not wait behind
            // hundreds of unrelated enum tuples. This is only a candidate;
            // ordinary validation and engine replay still decide refutation.
            if sample==0 {
                fn pin(rule:&RuleExpr,row:&mut Value)->bool{match rule{
                    RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(f),RuleTerm::Literal(v))|RuleExpr::Ne(RuleTerm::Field(f),RuleTerm::Literal(v))|
                    RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(v),RuleTerm::Field(f))|RuleExpr::Ne(RuleTerm::Literal(v),RuleTerm::Field(f))=>{
                        if let Value::Row(fields)=row&&fields.contains_key(f){fields.insert(f.clone(),v.clone());true}else{false}
                    },
                    RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>xs.iter().fold(false,|any,r|pin(r,row)||any),
                    RuleExpr::Not(r)=>pin(r,row),_=>false,
                }}
                if !inv.policy.as_ref().zip(rows.get_mut(&inv.table).and_then(|rs|rs.first_mut())).is_some_and(|(p,row)|pin(p,row)){continue;}
            }
            let candidate_args=inv.operation.as_ref().and_then(|op|bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==op)).and_then(|p|arguments(&p.program.args,variant));
            // Pruned dependencies can repeat after unrelated schema enum digits.
            // Identical seed/typed-argument candidates are one search input.
            if !seen.insert(serde_json::to_string(&(&rows,&candidate_args)).unwrap()){continue;}
            let mut seeded=match engine(bound,&rows,program("$security.seed",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Get(Read{site:"security read".into(),table:inv.table.clone(),index:"byId".into(),key:vec![Expr::Literal(id(&inv.table))],provenance:KeyProvenance::Computed}))])){Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
            let rows=match readback(&seeded){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
            let Some(row)=rows.get(&inv.table).and_then(|r|r.first())else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: no generated ordinary row",inv.table));break;};
            for viewer in [VIEWER,OTHER,"account:security-no-role","service:security"]{
                if !out.reserve(1){break;}
                if inv.target=="always" {
                    match policy(bound,inv,&rows,viewer,None,row){
                        Ok(false)=>{out.attempted+=1;out.admitted+=1;out.violations.push(Counterexample{reachability:Reachability::TrustedSeedOnly,history:Vec::new(),observations:None,invariant_site:inv.site.to_string(),args:BTreeMap::new(),viewer:viewer.into(),operation:"$security.seed_at".into(),effect:"always".into(),row:row.clone(),old:None,supporting_rows:rows.clone(),rule_path:"trusted seed ingress; all typed and final-exists constraints rechecked".into(),scope:"actual seed_at commit/read-back; ordinary application reachability is not claimed".into()});found=true;},
                        Ok(true)=>{out.attempted+=1;},Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);}
                    }if found{break;}continue;
                }
                if let Some(operation)=&inv.operation {
                    let Some(p)=bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==operation)else{continue;};
                    let permitted=match policy(bound,inv,&rows,viewer,None,row){Ok(p)=>p,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{operation}: operation intent not judged: {why}"));continue;}};
                    let Some(args)=arguments(&p.program.args,variant)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{operation}: arguments need fixture assets or unsupported samples"));continue;};
                    if !out.reserve(1){break;}
                    out.attempted+=1;
                    let mut seeded=match engine(bound,&rows,program("$security.seed",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])){Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
                    let args=match materialize_arguments(&mut seeded,&p.program.args,&args,viewer){Ok(a)=>a,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{operation}: argument ledger unavailable: {why}"));continue;}};
                    match seeded.mutate(operation,None,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)) {
                        Ok(result) if matches!(result.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Sent{..})=>{
                            let current=readback(&seeded).unwrap_or_default();
                            let changed=current.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().filter(|r|!rows.get(&inv.table).is_some_and(|old|old.contains(r))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                            for next in changed {
                                let old=rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|old|old.field("id").ok()==next.field("id").ok());
                                // An upsert must be attributed to its executed arm;

fn engine_on_store(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,probes:Vec<Program>,store:Store)->Result<Engine,String>{
    let mut programs=bound.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>();programs.extend(probes);
    let mut engine=Engine::new_at(store,bound.schema.clone(),programs,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    let mut seeded=rows.iter().filter(|(name,_)|bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_none()).map(|(name,rs)|{
        let t=&bound.schema.tables[name];
        let clean=rs.iter().map(|r|if let Value::Row(fields)=r {Value::Row(fields.iter().filter(|(c,_)|!t.engine_field(c)&&!t.recorded.contains_key(*c)&&!t.maintained.contains_key(*c)).map(|(c,v)|(c.clone(),v.clone())).collect())}else{r.clone()}).collect();
        (name.clone(),clean)
    }).collect::<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>>();
    for (table,values) in &mut seeded {for row in values {for (column,kind) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns {
        if let Ok(v)=row.field(column) {
            let value=hydrate(&mut engine,kind,v)?;
            if let Value::Row(fields)=row {fields.insert(column.clone(),value);}
        }
    }}}
    engine.seed_at(seeded.iter().flat_map(|(t,rs)|rs.iter().map(move|r|(t.clone(),r.clone()))).collect::<Vec<_>>(),NOW).map_err(|e|format!("generated snapshot rejected by schema: {e}"))?;
    validate_fixture(bound,&mut engine)?;
    Ok(engine)
}
fn validate_fixture(bound:&Bound,engine:&mut Engine)->Result<(),String>{
    let actual=readback(engine)?;
    for (name,values) in &actual {for row in values {
        snapback4_core::engine::validate::validate_row(&bound.schema,name,row).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        for c in &bound.schema.tables[name].constraints {if let Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert{table,index,key}=c {
            let rule=RuleExpr::Exists{table:table.clone(),index:index.clone(),key:key.clone(),provenance:KeyProvenance::Computed,equalities:Vec::new()};
            if engine.fixture_policy(&rule,snapback4_core::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL,None,row,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=rules::Verdict::Readable {return Err(format!("{name}: trusted seed did not establish final-exists constraint"));}
        }}
    }}
    Ok(())
}

    let name="$security.probe";let table=inv.table.clone();let key=id(&table);
    let read=Read{site:"security challenge".into(),table:table.clone(),index:"byId".into(),key:vec![Expr::Literal(key.clone())],provenance:KeyProvenance::Computed};

/// The prefix of the reference index a live child of `parent` is found under.
pub const REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX: &str = "$reference:";
pub fn reference_index_name(column: &str) -> String { format!("{REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX}{column}") }

/// Heap a closure keeps per reached row: its table and id (an id is at most
/// 144 canonical bytes) and the set and queue entries holding them.
pub const CLOSURE_IDENTITY_BYTES: u64 = 384;
/// Child rows a cascade or set-null walk reads per seek (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
pub const REFERENCE_PAGE: usize = 4;

impl Schema {
    /// Whether rows of `name` take part in referential actions, as child or
    /// parent: an ordinary durable application table. Ephemeral rows, engine
    /// derivations and receipt/timeline bookkeeping keep their own lifetimes.
    pub fn actionable(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
        let Some(table) = self.table(name) else { return false };
        table.ephemeral.is_none() && self.maintain_for(name).is_none() && self.private_owner(name).is_none()
            && !self.is_recount_work(name) && !self.timelines.iter().any(|t| t.owns(name))
            && !self.receipts.iter().any(|f| [&f.scope, &f.members, &f.runs].iter().any(|t| t.as_str() == name))
    }

    /// Every reference into `parent` a delete consults (all but `keep`), in
    /// (child, column) order.
    pub fn referencing<'a>(&'a self, parent: &str) -> Vec<Referencing<'a>> {
        // A plain loop into a vector: iterator adapters monomorphize per call site on the web device.
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        for (child, table) in &self.tables {
            for (column, action) in &table.on_delete {
                if action.acts() && matches!(table.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: target }) if target == parent) {
                    out.push(Referencing { child, table, column, action: *action });
                }
            }
        }

/// Why `column` cannot be cleared by its parent's delete, if it cannot: a
/// value that carries authority or meaning beyond "which parent this row has".
// @ref LLP 3000.039#2.2 — set null never changes any rule's verdict, moves a row between audiences, or rewrites a fact resolved through the reference.
pub fn set_null_refusal(schema: &Schema, name: &str, table: &Table, column: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let audience = table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|sync| std::iter::once((&sync.audience, sync.horizon.as_ref()))
        .chain(sync.also.iter().map(|arm| (&arm.audience, arm.horizon.as_ref())))
        .any(|(audience, horizon)| match audience {
        Audience::Public => false,
        Audience::Column(c) => c == column,
        Audience::Target { prefix, .. } => prefix.iter().any(|c| c == column),
    } || horizon.is_some_and(|h| table.index(&h.by).is_some_and(|i| i.uses_column(column)))));
    let maintained = schema.maintains.iter().any(|m| m.source == name && m.by.iter().any(|by| by.trim_start_matches('.').starts_with(&format!("{column}."))));
    if !table.columns.get(column).is_some_and(ColumnType::is_optional) { Some("the reference is required; declare it optional (`?`) or choose cascade".into()) }
    else if table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == column) { Some("a read-through reference is required and immutable".into()) }
    else if table.immutable_fields().any(|f| f == column) { Some("the column is immutable".into()) }
    else if table.written_by.contains_key(column) { Some("the column is written by its field owner".into()) }
    else if table.sequences.values().any(|p| p == column) { Some("a sequence counts within it".into()) }
    else if table.recorded.values().any(|r| r.reference == column) || maintained { Some("a recorded or maintained path resolves through it".into()) }
    else if carries_authority(schema, name, column) { Some("a rule reads it, so clearing it would change that rule's verdict; a reference that carries authority chooses cascade, restrict or keep".into()) }
    else if audience { Some("the sync audience or horizon uses it, so clearing it would move the row between partitions".into()) }
    else if table.constraints.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Constraint::Cap { by, .. } if by.iter().any(|b| b == column) && by.len() != 1)) { Some(format!("a cap groups by it with other columns; its only cap is `cap N by {column}`")) }
    else { None }
}

/// Why rows of `name` cannot be deleted or cleared by another row's delete.
pub fn write_target_refusal(schema: &Schema, name: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let table = schema.table(name)?;
    if table.constraints.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Constraint::AppendOnly)) { Some(format!("{name} is append only")) }
    else if !table.written_by.is_empty() { Some(format!("{name} has field-owned (`written by`) columns, whose owners the schema singled out")) }
    else if schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m| m.source == name || m.row_key.as_ref().is_some_and(|k| k.table == name))
        .flat_map(|m| [Some(m.target.as_str()), m.private.as_ref().map(|owner| owner.table.as_str())]).flatten()
        .any(|destination| schema.table(destination).is_some_and(|t| t.sync.is_some() && t.written_by.values().any(|authority| !authority.audience))) {
        Some(format!("{name}'s derived writes deliver private fields, whose recipient authorization has no fixed action price"))
    }
    else if schema.timeline_member(name) { Some(format!("a timeline orders {name}'s writes")) }
    else if schema.maintains.iter().any(|m| m.row_key.as_ref().is_some_and(|k| k.table == name)) { Some(format!("{name} is a row-keyed view's member table, whose writes may recount a group outside any fixed price")) }
    else if schema.maintains.iter().any(|m| m.source == name && matches!(m.kind, MaintainKind::Sum { .. })) { Some(format!("{name} feeds a maintained sum: system work can delete rows despite `delete <- deny`, and a cascade from another parent can reach them; a hidden contribution could push the sum out of range, so whether the delete commits would depend on rows the deleter cannot see")) }
    else if schema.write_reservation(name).is_none_or(|plan| plan.server_only) { Some(format!("{name}'s writes have no fixed price, so a cascade into it cannot be reserved")) }
    else { None }
}

fn add(to: &mut Price, price: Price) {
    for (to, price) in to.iter_mut().zip(price) { *to = to.saturating_add(price); }
}

/// Fixture-free campaigns retain empty-start bootstraps and populated role worlds.
/// The two initial states share the existing 512-single/512-pair work envelope.
pub fn generated_campaign(bound:&Bound,campaign:&mut Campaign,matrix:bool){
    campaign.history_limit=64;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
    if !campaign.reserve(1){return;}
    match engine_with(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),Vec::new()){
        Ok(empty)=>fixture_observers(bound,&empty,campaign),
        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("empty initial campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
    }
    campaign.history_limit=448;campaign.generated_fixture=true;
    match generated_fixture(bound,campaign){
        Ok(fixture)=>if matrix{operations(bound,&fixture,campaign)}else{fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,campaign)},
        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
    }
    campaign.history_limit=0;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
}
/// Exercise every authored query/mutation with cross-principal candidates.
/// This is conformance exploration, not a guessed spec oracle: only declared
/// policies and replayed violations can fail security acceptance.
pub fn fixture_observers(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
    let first=campaign.violations.len();
    histories::initial_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
    column_reads::fixture(bound,fixture,campaign);
    histories::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
    if campaign.generated_fixture {for witness in &mut campaign.violations[first..] {
        if witness.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace {witness.reachability=Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace;witness.scope="actual authored observation from a validated generated initial fixture; fixture initialization is not claimed application-reachable".into();}
    }}
}
pub fn operations(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
    fixture_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
    assets::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
    let fixtures=match readback(fixture){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(e);return;}};
    let has_fixture=fixtures.values().any(|r|!r.is_empty());
    if fixtures.values().map(Vec::len).sum::<usize>()>1000{campaign.unsupported.insert("operation fixture matrix exceeds 1000 rows; provide a focused security fixture".into());return;}
    let mut viewers=histories::viewers(bound);
    for (table,rows) in &fixtures{for row in rows{for (c,k) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns{if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)&&let Ok(Value::Id(p))=row.field(c)&&!viewers.contains(p){viewers.push(p.clone());}}}}
    let eligible=bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|matches!(p.program.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)&&!p.program.native).count();
    let quota=(8192/eligible.max(1)).clamp(1,512);let mut remaining=8192usize;
    for p in &bound.programs{
        if !matches!(p.program.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)||p.program.native{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: trusted/system/opaque operation",p.program.name));continue;}
        let target=bound.schema.visible_tables().find(|(t,_)|super::audit::mentions(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),t)).or_else(||bound.schema.visible_tables().next()).map(|(t,_)|t.clone());
        let Some(target)=target else{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: no table population for generated arguments",p.program.name));continue;};
        let dummy=Invariant{table:target,target:"read".into(),column:None,predicate:"allow".into(),site:crate::Site::default(),policy:Some(RuleExpr::Allow),operation:Some(p.program.name.clone()),requirements:Vec::new(),syntax:None,unsupported:None};
        let cases=histories::fixture_cases(&p.program,&fixtures,&viewers,campaign);
        let mut exercised=0;
        'cases:for (variant,mut args) in cases.into_iter().enumerate(){
            let rows=if has_fixture{fixtures.clone()}else{match snapshot(bound,&dummy,variant){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {e}",p.program.name));break;}}};
            if !has_fixture{for (arg,kind) in &p.program.args {if let ir::ArgType::Ref{table}=kind&&let Some(row)=rows.get(table).and_then(|rs|rs.first())&&let Ok(id)=row.field("id"){args.insert(arg.clone(),id.clone());}}}
            for viewer in &viewers{
                if exercised==quota||remaining==0{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: operation matrix capped at {quota} calls to share the 8192-call budget across every operation; remaining arguments/viewers not covered",p.program.name));break 'cases;}
                exercised+=1;remaining-=1;
                let mut engine=match if has_fixture {fork(bound,fixture,Vec::new())}else{engine(bound,&rows,program("$security.matrix",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))]))}{Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {e}",p.program.name));continue;}};
                let args=match materialize_arguments(&mut engine,&p.program.args,&args,viewer){Ok(a)=>a,Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: argument ledger unavailable: {why}",p.program.name));continue;}};
                campaign.attempted+=1;*campaign.operations.entry(p.program.name.clone()).or_default()+=1;
                if p.program.kind==ProgramKind::Query{
                    match engine.query(&p.program.name,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)){Ok(_)=>{},Err(e) if e.code=="E_INPUT"=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: generated arguments rejected as E_INPUT",p.program.name));},Err(_)=>campaign.refused+=1,}
                }else{
                    match engine.mutate(&p.program.name,None,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)){Ok(result) if matches!(result.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Failed{..})=>campaign.refused+=1,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: matrix engine error {e}",p.program.name));},_=>{},}
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

fn fork(_bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,probes:Vec<Program>)->Result<Engine,String>{
    if !probes.is_empty(){return Err("fixture probes must be authored operations".into());}
    fixture.fork_fixture().map_err(|e|e.to_string())
}

+fn role_candidates(bound:&Bound,target:&str,row:&snapback4_core::schema::Table)->Vec<RuleExpr>{
+    use snapback4_core::{schema::ColumnType as C,Value};
+    let mut out=Vec::new();
+    for (roster,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){
+        let mut columns=super::privilege::role_columns(bound,roster);
+        columns.extend(["role","kind"].into_iter().filter(|c|t.columns.contains_key(*c)).map(str::to_owned));
+        for column in columns {for role in super::privilege::role_values(bound,roster,&column) {
+            for (index,idx) in t.indexes.iter().filter(|(_,idx)|idx.unique){
+                let mut key=Vec::new();let mut principals=0;
+                for component in &idx.components {
+                    let Some(c)=component.column()else{break;};
+                    let term=if c==&column{RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(role.clone()))}else{match t.columns.get(c).map(C::required){
+                        Some(C::Principal)=>{principals+=1;RuleTerm::Viewer},
+                        Some(C::Ref{table}) if table==target=>RuleTerm::Field("id".into()),
+                        Some(C::Ref{table})=>{
+                            let refs=row.columns.iter().filter(|(_,k)|matches!(k.required(),C::Ref{table:parent} if parent==table)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
+                            if refs.len()!=1{break;}RuleTerm::Field(refs[0].0.clone())
+                        },_=>break,
+                    }};key.push(term);
+                }
+                if principals!=1||key.len()!=idx.components.len(){continue;}
+                let equalities=if idx.components.iter().any(|c|c.column()==Some(&column)){Vec::new()}else{vec![(column.clone(),RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(role.clone())))]};
+                let policy=RuleExpr::Exists{table:roster.clone(),index:index.clone(),key,provenance:snapback4_core::ir::KeyProvenance::Computed,equalities};
+                if !out.contains(&policy){out.push(policy);}break;
+            }
+        }}
+    }out

+/// Fixture-free campaigns retain empty-start bootstraps and populated role worlds.
+/// The two initial states share the existing 512-single/512-pair work envelope.
+pub fn generated_campaign(bound:&Bound,campaign:&mut Campaign,matrix:bool){
+    campaign.history_limit=64;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
+    if !campaign.reserve(1){return;}
+    match engine_with(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),Vec::new()){
+        Ok(empty)=>fixture_observers(bound,&empty,campaign),
+        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("empty initial campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
+    }
+    campaign.history_limit=448;campaign.generated_fixture=true;
+    match generated_fixture(bound,campaign){
+        Ok(fixture)=>if matrix{operations(bound,&fixture,campaign)}else{fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,campaign)},
+        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
+    }
+    campaign.history_limit=0;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
+}
 /// Exercise every authored query/mutation with cross-principal candidates.
 /// This is conformance exploration, not a guessed spec oracle: only declared
 /// policies and replayed violations can fail security acceptance.
 pub fn fixture_observers(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
+    let first=campaign.violations.len();
+    histories::initial_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
     column_reads::fixture(bound,fixture,campaign);
     histories::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
+    if campaign.generated_fixture {for witness in &mut campaign.violations[first..] {
+        if witness.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace {witness.reachability=Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace;witness.scope="actual authored observation from a validated generated initial fixture; fixture initialization is not claimed application-reachable".into();}
+    }}
 }

codex
I found a possible gap: the new removal seed checks `table.expire`, while runtime expiry also includes inherited read-through deadlines. A same-ID insert can retire an expired predecessor and run its reference actions. I’m checking whether that path can leave a policy dependency incorrectly classified as stable.
